p(\alpha)=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{y_{k}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)\theta_{k}.

For
\alpha\in[-\frac{\delta_{0}}{K},\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}]
, we now establish a quadratic
approximation for
p(\alpha)
. We have

p(\alpha)=\mathrm{I}(\alpha)+\mathrm{II}(\alpha)+\mathrm{III}(\alpha)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)\theta_{k}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)(\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*})

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)\theta_{k}.

For
\mathrm{I}(\alpha)
, observe that

\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\theta_{k}^{*}=|\theta_{k}^{*}|^{2}
is real, and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Re}e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)

\displaystyle=\cos(k\alpha)-\cos(k\alpha_{0})+k(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\sin(k\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle=-\frac{k^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}}{2}\cos(k\tilde{\alpha}),

for some
\tilde{\alpha}
between
\alpha
and
\alpha_{0}
.
Since
\alpha,\alpha_{0}\in[-\frac{\delta_{0}}{K},\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}]

and
k\leq K
, for sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
this implies

-\frac{k^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}}{4}\geq\operatorname{Re}e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)\geq-\frac{3k^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}}{4}.

Then, applying the second and third bounds of (
81
),
there are constants
C,c>0
(independent of
\delta_{0}
) such that

-cK^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}\geq\mathrm{I}(\alpha)\geq-CK^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}.

For
\mathrm{II}(\alpha)
,
we apply
|e^{is}-1-is|\leq s^{2}
for all real values
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
Cauchy-Schwarz, and the condition
\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})
to obtain

|\mathrm{II}(\alpha)|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}|\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}||\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*}|\leq(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}K^{2}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\theta_{k}^{*}|^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\theta_{k}-\theta_{k}^{*}|^{2}}\leq\delta_{1}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

For
\mathrm{III}(\alpha)
, we
apply the condition (
95
) for
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{F}_{2}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})
to obtain

|\mathrm{III}(\alpha)|\leq\delta_{1}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

Combining these bounds, for sufficiently small
\delta_{1}>0
and some constants

C_{0},c_{0}>0
which we may take independent of
\delta_{0},\delta_{1}
,
we arrive at the desired quadratic approximation

-c_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}\geq p(\alpha)\geq-C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}\qquad\text{ for }\alpha\in[-\tfrac{\delta_{0}}{K},\tfrac{\delta_{0}}{K}].

(99)

This implies the following variance bound: Denote
I_{1}=[-\frac{\delta_{0}}{K},\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}]
and
I_{2}=[-\pi,\pi)\setminus[-\frac{\delta_{0}}{K},\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}]
. For any bounded function

f:[-\pi,\pi)\to\mathbb{R}
, denote
\|f\|_{\infty}=\sup_{\alpha\in[-\pi,\pi)}|f(\alpha)|
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}[f(\alpha)]

\displaystyle=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}(f(\alpha)-x)^{2}e^{p(\alpha)/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}{\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{p(\alpha)/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\int_{I_{1}}(f(\alpha)-x)^{2}e^{p(\alpha)/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha+4\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{I_{2}}e^{p(\alpha)/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}{\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{p(\alpha)/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\int_{I_{1}}(f(\alpha)-x)^{2}e^{-c_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}{\int_{I_{1}}e^{-C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha}+4\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}e^{-c(\delta_{0})\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}

(100)

where, in the last line, we have used (
99
) as well as
(
91
) to bound
\mathbb{P}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}[\alpha\in I_{2}]\leq e^{-c(\delta_{0})\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
for a constant
c(\delta_{0})>0

depending on
\delta_{0}
. For the denominator of the first term of
(
100
), we may evaluate the Gaussian integral as

\displaystyle\int_{I_{1}}e^{-C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}\sqrt{2\pi}\left(1-\tilde{\Phi}\left[\sqrt{\frac{2C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}+\alpha_{0}\right)\right]-\tilde{\Phi}\left[\sqrt{\frac{2C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}\left(\frac{\delta_{0}}{K}-\alpha_{0}\right)\right]\right).

Here, since
|\alpha_{0}|\leq\delta_{0}/K
, both values of
\tilde{\Phi}
are at
most
1/2
. Furthermore, under the condition

\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
for
C_{1}>0
large enough depending on

\delta_{0}
, at least one value of
\tilde{\Phi}

is less than
1/4
. Thus, for a constant
C>0
independent of

\delta_{0},\delta_{1}
, we have simply

\int_{I_{1}}e^{-C_{0}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}/\sigma^{2}}d\alpha\geq\left(\frac{CK^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)^{-1/2}.

Combining this with the normalization constant for the Gaussian law in the
numerator of the first term of (
100
), we obtain

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}[f(\alpha)]\leq C\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[f(\alpha)]+4\|f\|_{\infty}^{2}e^{-c(\delta_{0})\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},\qquad\tau^{2}:=\frac{C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}}{K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}.

(101)

Here
C,C^{\prime}>0
are some constants depending only on
c_{\mathrm{gen}}
and
independent of
\delta_{0},\delta_{1}
, whereas
c(\delta_{0})
depends also on

\delta_{0}
.

Finally, we apply this bound (
101
) to the function

f(\alpha)=v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y=\langle y,g(\alpha)v\rangle
.
Observe that

\|f\|_{\infty}\leq\|\theta^{*}\|\|v\|+\sigma\sup_{\alpha\in[-\pi,\pi)}\langle\varepsilon,g(\alpha)v\rangle\leq C\|\theta^{*}\|,

(102)

the last inequality using
\|v\|=1
and (
94
) for
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{F}_{2}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})
. To bound

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[f(\alpha)]
, we apply
again the inner-product relation
(
13
) to write the complex representation of
f(\alpha)
as

f(\alpha)=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{y_{k}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha}v_{k}=\mathrm{I}(\alpha)+\mathrm{II}(\alpha)+\mathrm{III}(\alpha)

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(1+ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)v_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big(e^{ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})}-1-ik(\alpha-\alpha_{0})\Big)v_{k}

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha}v_{k}

Next, we are going to upper bound their variances under
\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})
. For
\mathrm{I}(\alpha)
, we may drop the constant term that is independent of

\alpha
and write

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\mathrm{I}(\alpha)]=\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}\left[\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot ik\alpha\cdot v_{k}+\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot(e^{ik\alpha_{0}}-1)ik\alpha\cdot v_{k}\right].

(103)

Recalling the tangent vector
u^{*}
from (
53
), observe that its
complex representation is

\tilde{u}^{*}=\frac{d}{d\alpha}\Big(e^{ik\alpha}\theta_{k}^{*}:k=1,\ldots,K\Big)\Big|_{\alpha=0}=\Big(ik\theta_{k}^{*}:k=1,\ldots,K\Big).

Then the inner-product relation (
13
) and the given
orthogonality condition
\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0
imply

\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}-ik\cdot\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot v_{k}=0,

so the first term inside the variance of (
103
) is 0. Applying

|e^{ik\alpha_{0}}-1|\leq k|\alpha_{0}|\leq\delta_{0}k/K
for the second term,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\mathrm{I}(\alpha)]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\alpha]\cdot\left(\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\theta_{k}^{*}}\cdot(e^{ik\alpha_{0}}-1)ik\cdot v_{k}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\alpha]\cdot\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\theta_{k}^{*}|\cdot\frac{\delta_{0}k^{2}}{K}\cdot|v_{k}|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\alpha]\cdot\delta_{0}^{2}K^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\theta^{*}_{k}|^{2}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{K}|v_{k}|^{2}=\tau^{2}\delta_{0}^{2}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

For
\mathrm{II}(\alpha)
, applying
|e^{is}-1-is|\leq s^{2}
for any real value

s\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\mathrm{II}(\alpha)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\mathrm{II}(\alpha)^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}\left[\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{2}(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}|\theta_{k}^{*}||v_{k}|\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{4}]K^{4}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|\theta_{k}^{*}|^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|v_{k}|^{2}=3\tau^{4}K^{4}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

For
\mathrm{III}(\alpha)
, we may center by a constant independent of
\alpha

and apply (
96
) to obtain

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[\mathrm{III}(\alpha)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}\left[\left(\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sigma\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\Big(e^{ik\alpha}-e^{ik\alpha_{0}}\Big)v_{k}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}]\cdot\frac{\delta_{1}^{2}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}=\tau^{2}\delta_{1}^{2}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

Combining all of the above, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{N}(\alpha_{0},\tau^{2})}[f(\alpha)]

\displaystyle\leq 3\operatorname{Var}[\mathrm{I}(\alpha)]+3\operatorname{Var}[\mathrm{II}(\alpha)]+3\operatorname{Var}[\mathrm{III}(\alpha)]

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{2}\cdot C(\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+\tau^{4}\cdot CK^{4}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}

(104)

for a constant
C>0
independent of
\delta_{0},\delta_{1}
.

Applying (
102
) and (
104
) and the value of

\tau^{2}
to (
101
), for a constant
C^{\prime}>0
independent of

\delta_{0},\delta_{1}
,

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}[f(\alpha)]\leq C^{\prime}\left(\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}e^{-c(\delta_{0})\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}\right)\sigma^{2}.

Then, choosing
\delta_{0},\delta_{1}>0
sufficiently small depending on
\eta
,
and applying
\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
for a sufficiently large
constant
C_{1}>0
depending on
\delta_{0}
and
\eta
, we obtain

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}[f(\alpha)]\leq\eta\sigma^{2}
as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.7
.

Applying (
76
) with
t=\delta_{1}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|\langle\varepsilon,g(\alpha)\cdot\theta\rangle|>\frac{\delta_{1}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma}\text{ and }\|\varepsilon\|\leq s\right]\leq\frac{C\sigma}{\|\theta^{*}\|}\cdot Ks\cdot e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}

for constants
C,c>0
(depending on
\delta_{1}
). Let us take

s=\max(\sqrt{4K},\|\theta^{*}\|/\sigma).

Then applying
\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
for sufficiently large

C_{1}>0
, this probability bound is at most
e^{-c^{\prime}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
. By a
chi-squared tail bound, since
\|\varepsilon\|^{2}\sim\chi_{2K}^{2}
and

s^{2}\geq 4K
, we have

\mathbb{P}[\|\varepsilon\|^{2}>s^{2}]\leq e^{-cs^{2}}\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.
Combining these bounds gives, for a constant
c>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|\langle\varepsilon,g(\alpha)\cdot\theta\rangle|>\frac{\delta_{1}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma}\right]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

so
\varepsilon\in\mathcal{F}_{1}(\theta,\delta_{1})
with probability at least

1-e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.
The same argument applied with the unit vector
v
in place of
\theta
shows

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}|\langle\varepsilon,g(\alpha)\cdot v\rangle|>\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|}{\sigma}\right]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

so (
94
) holds with probability at least

1-e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.

For the condition (
95
),
note that
\varepsilon_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(0,2)
and
these are independent for
k=1,\ldots,K
. Then

f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon):=\alpha^{-2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\alpha}-1-ik\alpha)\theta_{k}

has distribution
\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(0,2\tau^{2})
where

\tau^{2}=\alpha^{-4}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\alpha}-1-ik\alpha)\theta_{k}|^{2}.

So
\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\tau^{2})
. Applying

|e^{is}-1-is|\leq s^{2}
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\tau^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{4}|\theta_{k}|^{2}\leq K^{4}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}.

Then setting

t=\frac{\delta_{1}K^{2}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma},

a Gaussian tail bound yields, for a constant
c>0
(depending on
\delta_{1}
),

\mathbb{P}[|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|>t/2]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}.

Differentiating in
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
and applying

|e^{is}-1-is|\leq s^{2}
and
|e^{is}-1-is+s^{2}/2|\leq|s|^{3}
for
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we
have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\alpha^{\prime}}\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle=\left|\alpha^{-2}\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot ike^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\alpha}-1-ik\alpha)\theta_{k}\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{3}|\varepsilon_{k}||\theta_{k}|\leq K^{3}\|\varepsilon\|\|\theta\|,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\alpha}\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle=\left|\alpha^{-3}\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\alpha}(ik\alpha-2)+2+ik\alpha)\theta_{k}\right|\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{3}|\varepsilon_{k}||\theta_{k}|\leq CK^{3}\|\varepsilon\|\|\theta\|.

(For the second line, we have explicitly evaluated the derivative,
and then applied
e^{ik\alpha}=1+ik\alpha-k^{2}\alpha^{2}/2+O(k^{3}\alpha^{3})

and canceled terms to yield the first inequality.)
Then, on an event
\{\|\varepsilon\|<s\}
,
\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)
is

L
-Lipschitz in both
\alpha
and
\alpha^{\prime}
, for

L=C_{0}K^{3}\|\theta^{*}\|s
and a constant
C_{0}>0
.
We set
\delta=t/(4L)
and let
N_{\delta}
be a
\delta
-net of

[-\pi,\pi)
having cardinality

|N_{\delta}|=\frac{8\pi L}{t}\leq\frac{C\sigma}{\|\theta^{*}\|}\cdot Ks.

Then

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[-\pi,\pi)}|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|>t\text{ and }\|\varepsilon\|<s\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in N_{\delta}}|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|>t/2\right]\leq|N_{\delta}|^{2}\cdot e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}.

Then, setting
s=\max(\sqrt{4K},\|\theta^{*}\|/\sigma)
and applying the same
argument as above shows

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[-\pi,\pi)}|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}(\varepsilon)|>t\right]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

so (
95
) holds with probability at least

1-e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.

The argument for (
96
) is analogous. We define

\gamma=\alpha-\alpha^{\prime}
,

f_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}(\varepsilon):=\gamma^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\gamma}-1)v_{k}\sim\mathcal{N}_{\mathbb{C}}(0,2\tau^{2}),\qquad\tau^{2}:=\gamma^{-2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}|(e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\gamma}-1)v_{k}|^{2}

and set
t=\delta_{1}K\|\theta^{*}\|/\sigma
. Applying
|e^{ik\gamma}-1|\leq k|\gamma|
and
\|v\|=1
,
we have
\tau^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{2}|v_{k}|^{2}\leq K^{2}
, so that a Gaussian tail
bound yields
\mathbb{P}[|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}(\varepsilon)|>t/2]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.
On the event
\{\|\varepsilon\|<s\}
, we have the Lipschitz bounds

\displaystyle|\partial_{\alpha^{\prime}}\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}(\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle=\left|\gamma^{-1}\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot ike^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\gamma}-1)v_{k}\right|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{2}|\varepsilon_{k}||v_{k}|\leq K^{2}\|\varepsilon\|\|v\|\leq K^{2}s,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\gamma}\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}(\varepsilon)|

\displaystyle=\left|\gamma^{-2}\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\overline{\varepsilon_{k}}\cdot e^{ik\alpha^{\prime}}(e^{ik\gamma}(ik\gamma-1)+1)v_{k}\right|\leq C\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{2}|\varepsilon_{k}||v_{k}|\leq CK^{2}\|\varepsilon\|\|v\|\leq CK^{2}s,

where we have applied
e^{ik\gamma}=1+ik\gamma+O(k^{2}\gamma^{2})
. Then
applying a covering net argument as above, whose details we omit for brevity,
we obtain

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\alpha^{\prime}\in[-\pi,\pi),\;\gamma\in[-2\pi,2\pi)}|\operatorname{Re}f_{\alpha^{\prime},\gamma}(\varepsilon)|>t\right]\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

so (
96
) holds with probability at least

1-e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
. Combining these bounds yields the lemma.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.5
.

Throughout the proof,
C,C^{\prime},c,c^{\prime}
etc. are positive constants depending only on

c_{\mathrm{gen}},\eta
and changing from instance to instance.
Recall the expression (
54
).
Let
C_{1},\delta_{1}>0
be such that the conclusion of Lemma
5.6

holds with
\eta/6
in place of
\eta
.
For any
\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})
and unit vector
v

satisfying
\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0
, let us apply

\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}\Big[v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}(\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon)\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon}}\Big[\big(v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}(\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon)\big)^{2}\Big]\leq\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon\|^{2}

to upper-bound the second term of (
54
) as

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}}\Big[v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}(\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)})\Big]\leq I_{1}(\theta,v)+I_{2}(\theta,v)+I_{3}

(105)

where

\displaystyle I_{1}(\theta,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\operatorname{Var}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}}\Big[v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}(\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)})\Big]\cdot\mathbf{1}[\varepsilon^{(m)}\in\mathcal{E}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})],

\displaystyle I_{2}(\theta,v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left[\varepsilon^{(m)}\notin\mathcal{E}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})\text{ and }\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\leq 4K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left[\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}>4K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right].

Here
I_{1},I_{2}
are dependent on
(\theta,v)
, whereas
I_{3}
is independent of

(\theta,v)
.

Lemma
5.6
applied with
\eta/6
immediately gives
the deterministic bound

I_{1}(\theta,v)\leq\eta\sigma^{2}/6.

(106)

For
I_{2}(\theta,v)
, on the event
\|\varepsilon\|^{2}\leq 4K+\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}
, we have
for a constant
C_{2}>0
that

\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon\|^{2}\leq 2\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+2\sigma^{2}\|\varepsilon\|^{2}\leq C_{2}(\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+K\sigma^{2}).

Thus

I_{2}(\theta,v)\leq C_{2}(\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+K\sigma^{2})\cdot\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}[\varepsilon^{(m)}\notin\mathcal{E}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})].

Denote
p=\mathbb{P}[\varepsilon^{(m)}\notin\mathcal{E}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})]
and

q=\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6C_{2}(\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+K\sigma^{2})}
. By Lemma
5.7
,

p\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}},

so in particular
p<q
for
\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
and
sufficiently large
C_{1}>0
. Then by a Chernoff bound for binomial random
variables
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 2.3.1)
,

\mathbb{P}[I_{2}(\theta,v)\geq\eta\sigma^{2}/6]=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{m=1}^{N}\mathbf{1}[\varepsilon^{(m)}\notin\mathcal{E}(\theta,v,\delta_{1})]\geq Nq\right]\leq\left(\frac{ep}{q}\right)^{Nq}.

We have
(ep/q)\leq e^{-c^{\prime}\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
when

\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
for sufficiently large
C_{1}>0
,
so this yields

\mathbb{P}[I_{2}(\theta,v)\geq\eta\sigma^{2}/6]\leq e^{-\frac{cN}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}.

(107)

For
I_{3}
, we bound separately its mean and its concentration.
Denote the summand of
I_{3}
as

z^{(m)}=\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}\left[\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}>4K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right].

Let
p^{\prime}=\mathbb{P}[\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}>4K+\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}]
. Then applying
\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\sim\chi_{2K}^{2}
and a chi-squared tail bound,

p^{\prime}\leq e^{-c(2K+\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2})}\leq e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/\sigma^{2}}
.
So by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\mathbb{E}[z^{(m)}]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{4}\right]}\cdot\sqrt{p^{\prime}}\leq C(\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+K\sigma^{2})\cdot e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}\leq\eta\sigma^{2}/12

(108)

the last inequality holding for
\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
and
sufficiently large
C_{1}>0
. For the concentration, let

\|X\|_{\psi_{1}}=\inf\{s>0:\mathbb{E}[e^{|X|/s}]\leq 2\}
denote the
sub-exponential norm of a random variable
X
. Observe that similarly by
Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{|z^{(m)}|}{s}}\Big]

\displaystyle=1-p^{\prime}+\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{\|\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}}{s}\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}\left[\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}>4K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\Big[\exp\Big(4\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}+4\sigma^{2}\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2})/s\Big)\Big]}\cdot\sqrt{p^{\prime}}.

Applying the moment generating function bound

\mathbb{E}[\exp(t\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2})]=(1-2t)^{-K}\leq e^{4tK}
for
t<1/4
, we have

\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{|z^{(m)}|}{s}}\Big]\leq 1+e^{2\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/s}\cdot e^{8K\sigma^{2}/s}\cdot e^{-c\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}\leq 2

when
s\geq C^{\prime}\sigma^{2}(1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2})
for a sufficiently large constant

C^{\prime}>0
. So
\|z^{(m)}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C\sigma^{2}(1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2})
,
and Bernstein’s inequality
(
Vershynin 2018
, Theorem 2.8.1)
gives

\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}z^{(m)}-\mathbb{E}[z^{(m)}]>\eta\sigma^{2}/12\right]\leq e^{-\frac{cN}{(1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2})^{2}}}.

Combining with (
108
),

\mathbb{P}[I_{3}\geq\eta\sigma^{2}/6]=\mathbb{P}\left[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{m=1}^{N}z^{(m)}>\eta\sigma^{2}/6\right]\leq e^{-\frac{cN}{(1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2})^{2}}}.

(109)

Applying (
54
), (
105
),
(
106
), and (
107
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\leq\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\eta}{2\sigma^{2}}\text{ and }I_{3}\leq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[I_{1}(\theta,v)+I_{2}(\theta,v)+I_{3}\geq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{2}\text{ and }I_{3}\leq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[I_{2}(\theta,v)\geq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}\right]\leq e^{-\frac{cN}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}.

(110)

Let us apply a covering net argument to take a union bound over
(\theta,v)
,
and then combine with the bound (
109
) for
I_{3}
which
is independent of
(\theta,v)
.
We compute the Lipschitz constant of
v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v
in both
v
and
\theta
: Taking the gradient in
v
,

\left\|\nabla_{v}\Big[v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\Big]\right\|=\left\|2\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\right\|=2\sup_{u:\|u\|=1}u^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v.

Then denoting
y^{(m)}=\theta^{*}+\sigma\varepsilon^{(m)}
and applying (
45
),

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{v}\Big[v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\Big]\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\sup_{u:\|u\|=1}\frac{u^{\top}v}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{1}{N\sigma^{4}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\operatorname{Cov}_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}}[u^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y^{(m)},v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y^{(m)}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{2}{N\sigma^{4}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|y^{(m)}\|^{2}\leq\frac{2}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{4\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{4}}+\frac{4}{N\sigma^{2}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}.

(111)

Similarly, taking the gradient in
\theta
,

\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\left[v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\right]\right\|=\sup_{u:\|u\|=1}\nabla^{3}R_{N}(\theta)[u,v,v]

where
\nabla^{3}R_{N}(\theta)[u,v,v]
is the 3rd-derivative tensor of

R_{N}(\theta)
evaluated at
u\otimes v\otimes v\in\mathbb{R}^{2K\times 2K\times 2K}
. We have

\nabla^{3}R_{N}(\theta)[u,v,v]=-\frac{1}{N\sigma^{6}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\kappa_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}}^{3}[u^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y^{(m)},v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y^{(m)},v^{\top}g(\alpha)^{-1}y^{(m)}]

where
\kappa_{\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}}^{3}[\cdot,\cdot,\cdot]

denotes the 3rd-order mixed cumulant with respect to
\alpha\sim\mathcal{P}_{\theta,\varepsilon^{(m)}}
. For any random variables
X,Y,Z
,
the moment-cumulant relations and Hölder’s inequality give

|\kappa^{3}[X,Y,Z]|\leq C\cdot\mathbb{E}[|X|^{3}]^{1/3}\mathbb{E}[|Y|^{3}]^{1/3}\mathbb{E}[|Z|^{3}]^{1/3}.

Thus,

\left\|\nabla_{\theta}\left[v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\right]\right\|\leq\frac{C}{N\sigma^{6}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|y^{(m)}\|^{3}\leq\frac{4C\|\theta^{*}\|^{3}}{\sigma^{6}}+\frac{4C}{N\sigma^{3}}\sum_{m=1}^{N}\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{3}.

(112)

On an event

\mathcal{A}=\left\{\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}\leq N\text{ for all }m=1,\ldots,N\right\},

(
111
) and (
112
) imply that
v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v
is
L_{v}
-Lipschitz in
v
and
L_{\theta}
-Lipschitz in
\theta
,
for

L_{v}=C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+N\right)\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}},\qquad L_{\theta}=C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+N\right)^{3/2}\frac{1}{\sigma^{3}}.

Let
N_{v}
be a
\delta_{v}
-net of
\{v:\|v\|=1,\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0\}
and
N_{\theta}
a
\delta_{\theta}
-net of
\{\theta:\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})\}
, for
\delta_{v}=\eta/(4L_{v}\sigma^{2})
and

\delta_{\theta}=\eta/(4L_{\theta}\sigma^{2})
. This guarantees, for each

\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})
and unit vector
v
with
\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0
,
there exists
(\theta^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in N_{\theta}\times N_{v}
such that

\Big|v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v-{v^{\prime}}^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta^{\prime})v^{\prime}\Big|\leq L_{\theta}\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|+L_{v}\|v-v^{\prime}\|\leq\eta/2\sigma^{2}.

Then, applying these Lipschitz
bounds together with the pointwise bound (
110
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})}\,\sup_{v:\|v\|=1,\,\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0}\,v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\leq\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\eta}{\sigma^{2}}\text{ and }I_{3}\leq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}\text{ and }\mathcal{A}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in N_{\theta}}\,\sup_{v\in N_{v}}v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\leq\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\eta}{2\sigma^{2}}\text{ and }I_{3}\leq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}\right]\leq|N_{v}|\cdot|N_{\theta}|\cdot e^{-\frac{cN}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}.

(113)

We may take the above nets to have cardinalities

\displaystyle|N_{v}|\leq\left(1+\frac{2}{\delta_{v}}\right)^{2K}

\displaystyle\leq\left[C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+N\right)\right]^{2K},

\displaystyle|N_{\theta}|\leq\left(1+\frac{C\|\theta^{*}\|}{\delta_{\theta}}\right)^{2K}

\displaystyle\leq\left[C^{\prime}\left(\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+N\right)^{3/2}\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|}{\sigma}\right]^{2K}.

Observe that under the given assumptions
N\geq C_{0}K(1+\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}})\log(K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}})
and
\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq C_{1}\log K
, we have

\frac{N}{\log N}\geq\frac{C_{0}K(1+\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}})\log(K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}})}{\log[C_{0}K(1+\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}})\log(K+\frac{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}})]}.

Considering separately the cases
\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}\leq 1

and
1\leq\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}\leq\frac{K}{C_{1}\log K}
,
it may be checked that this implies

\frac{N}{\log N}\geq C_{0}^{\prime}K\left(1+\frac{K\sigma^{2}}{\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}\right)

(114)

where
C_{0}^{\prime}
may be taken to be a small absolute constant times
C_{0}/\log C_{0}
.
Then for sufficiently large
C_{0},C_{0}^{\prime}>0
and some constant
c^{\prime}>0
, the right side of
(
113
) may then be bounded as

|N_{v}|\cdot|N_{\theta}|\cdot e^{-\frac{cN}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}\leq e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}N}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}
.

Combining this with (
109
) and the chi-squared tail bound
\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}^{c}]\leq N\cdot\mathbb{P}[\|\varepsilon^{(m)}\|^{2}>N]\leq Ne^{-cN}\leq e^{-c^{\prime}N}
for
N\geq C_{0}K
, this gives

\mathbb{P}\left[\sup_{\theta\in\mathcal{B}(\delta_{1})}\,\sup_{v:\|v\|=1,\,\langle u^{*},v\rangle=0}\,v^{\top}\nabla^{2}R_{N}(\theta)v\leq\frac{1-\eta}{\sigma^{2}}\right]\leq e^{-\frac{c^{\prime}N}{1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2}}}+\mathbb{P}[I_{3}>\tfrac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{6}]+\mathbb{P}[\mathcal{A}^{c}]\leq e^{-\frac{cN}{(1+K\sigma^{2}/\|\theta^{*}\|^{2})^{2}}}

which implies the lemma.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs for minimax lower bound

For expositional clarity, we first show Lemma
6.1

in the setting
\beta=0
where the Fourier coefficients do not decay. At the
conclusion of this section, we extend the proof to all
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

The first bound (
58
) is basic and due to
the data processing inequality: Let
q_{\theta}(\alpha,y)
denote the joint
density of
\alpha\sim\operatorname{Unif}([-\pi,\pi))
and
y=g(\alpha)\cdot\theta+\sigma\varepsilon
. Then the data processing inequality implies

\displaystyle D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})\leq D_{\mathrm{KL}}(q_{\theta}\|q_{\theta^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(\alpha,y)\sim q_{\theta}}\left[-\frac{\|y-g(\alpha)\cdot\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\|y-g(\alpha)\cdot\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha\sim\operatorname{Unif}([-\pi,\pi))}}_{\varepsilon\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I)}\left[-\frac{\|\sigma\varepsilon\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\|g(\alpha)\cdot(\theta-\theta^{\prime})+\sigma\varepsilon\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right]=\frac{\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}.

In the remainder of the proof, we show (
59
). Let us write

\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\sim\operatorname{Unif}([-\pi,\pi))
for independent
random rotations,
\mathbb{E}
for the expectation over only
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
(fixing

y
and
\varepsilon
), and
g=g(\alpha)
and
g^{\prime}=g(\alpha^{\prime})
. We have

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})\leq\chi^{2}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2K}}\frac{[p_{\theta}(y)-p_{\theta^{\prime}}(y)]^{2}}{p_{\theta}(y)}dy

where the right side is the
\chi^{2}
-divergence,
see e.g.
(
Tsybakov 2008
, Lemma 2.7)
. We derive an upper bound
for
\chi^{2}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})
using the idea of

(
Bandeira et al. 2020
, Theorem 9)
: Let
\varphi(z)=(2\pi\sigma^{2})^{-K}\exp(-\|z\|^{2}/2\sigma^{2})
be the density of

\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{2K})
. Then

p_{\theta}(y)=\mathbb{E}[\varphi(y-g\theta)]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(y)\cdot e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\Big].

(115)

By Jensen’s inequality and the condition
\mathbb{E}[g]=0
,

p_{\theta}(y)\geq\varphi(y)\cdot e^{\frac{y^{\top}\mathbb{E}[g]\theta}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}=\varphi(y)\cdot e^{-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}.

(116)

Then applying (
115
), (
116
), and the
moment generating function

\int\varphi(y)e^{\frac{y^{\top}(g\theta+g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime})}{\sigma^{2}}}dy=e^{\frac{\|g\theta+g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}=e^{\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}+\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}+2\langle g\theta,g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}\rangle}{2\sigma^{2}}},

we get

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\leq\int\frac{\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(y)\cdot e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\Big]-\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(y)\cdot e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}}-\frac{\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\Big]\Big)^{2}}{\varphi(y)\cdot e^{-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}}dy

\displaystyle=\int\varphi(y)\Big(e^{-\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{y^{\top}g^{\prime}\theta}{\sigma^{2}}}\Big]-2e^{-\frac{\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{y^{\top}g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}}}\Big]+e^{-\frac{\|\theta^{\prime}\|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[e^{\frac{y^{\top}g\theta^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}}+\frac{y^{\top}g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}}{\sigma^{2}}}\Big]\Big)dy

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\langle g\theta,g^{\prime}\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}-2e^{\frac{\langle g\theta,g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}+e^{\frac{\langle g\theta^{\prime},g^{\prime}\theta^{\prime}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{\frac{\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}-2e^{\frac{\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}+e^{\frac{\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle}{\sigma^{2}}}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{\frac{\|\theta\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sigma^{2m}m!}\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{m}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}\big].

(117)

For
m=0
and
m=1
, the summand of (
117
) is 0.
We evaluate the summand for
m=2
, and upper bound it for
m\geq 3
. Let

\tilde{\theta}=(\theta_{1},\ldots,\theta_{K})\in\mathbb{C}^{K},\qquad\tilde{\theta}^{\prime}=(\theta_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\theta_{K}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}^{K}

be the complex representations of
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
as defined in Section

3.2
. For
m=2
, applying (
13
),

\mathbb{E}[\langle\theta,g(\alpha)\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{2}]=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\Big[\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\theta_{k_{1}}}e^{ik_{1}\alpha}\theta_{k_{1}}^{\prime})\cdot\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\theta_{k_{2}}}e^{ik_{2}\alpha}\theta_{k_{2}}^{\prime})\Big].

For any
k_{1},k_{2}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
, applying
\operatorname{Re}\bar{x}y=(x\bar{y}+\bar{x}y)/2
and
\mathbb{E}[e^{ik\alpha}]=0
for any non-zero integer
k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\theta_{k_{1}}}e^{ik_{1}\alpha}\theta_{k_{1}}^{\prime})\cdot\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\theta_{k_{2}}}e^{ik_{2}\alpha}\theta_{k_{2}}^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}\Big[(e^{-ik_{1}\alpha}\theta_{k_{1}}\overline{\theta_{k_{1}}^{\prime}}+e^{ik_{1}\alpha}\overline{\theta_{k_{1}}}\theta_{k_{1}}^{\prime})(e^{-ik_{2}\alpha}\theta_{k_{2}}\overline{\theta_{k_{2}}^{\prime}}+e^{ik_{2}\alpha}\overline{\theta_{k_{2}}}\theta_{k_{2}}^{\prime})\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbbm{1}\{k_{1}=k_{2}\}|\theta_{k_{1}}|^{2}|\theta_{k_{1}}^{\prime}|^{2}.

Then

\mathbb{E}[\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{2}]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}^{2}{r_{k}^{\prime}}^{2}
.
This identity holds also with
\theta=\theta^{\prime}
, so

\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{2}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{2}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{2}\big]=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}^{2}-{r_{k}^{\prime}}^{2})^{2}.

(118)

For any
m\geq 3
and every
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
,
applying again
\mathbb{E}[e^{ik\alpha}]=0
for
k\neq 0
, we have similarly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\theta_{k_{\ell}}}e^{ik_{\ell}\alpha}\theta_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{m}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}(e^{-ik_{\ell}\alpha}\theta_{k_{\ell}}\overline{\theta_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}}+e^{ik_{\ell}\alpha}\overline{\theta_{k_{\ell}}}\theta_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0\}\cdot\prod_{\ell:s_{\ell}=+1}\theta_{k_{\ell}}\overline{\theta_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}}\cdot\prod_{\ell:s_{\ell}=-1}\overline{\theta_{k_{\ell}}}\theta_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}

Noting that the left side is real and taking the real part on the right side,
this is equal to

\frac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0\}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)\cos\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right).

Then, summing over all
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}
and
applying this also for
\theta=\theta^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{m}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0\}\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\left[\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}-\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)^{2}+2\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)\left(1-\cos\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)\right)\right]=:\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II},

(119)

where
\mathrm{I}
is the term involving

(\prod_{\ell}r_{k_{\ell}}-\prod_{\ell}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime})^{2}
, and
\mathrm{II}
is the term
involving
\cos(\sum_{\ell}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime})
.

To upper bound
\mathrm{I}
, let us write

\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}-\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}(r_{k_{j}}-r_{k_{j}}^{\prime})r_{k_{1}}\ldots r_{k_{j-1}}r_{k_{j+1}}^{\prime}\ldots r_{k_{m}}^{\prime}.

Then

\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}-\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\leq m\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{m}(r_{k_{j}}-r_{k_{j}}^{\prime})^{2}\left(r_{k_{1}}\ldots r_{k_{j-1}}r_{k_{j+1}}^{\prime}\ldots r_{k_{m}}^{\prime}\right)^{2}.

So

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0\}\cdot\frac{m}{2^{m}}(r_{k_{j}}-r_{k_{j}}^{\prime})^{2}\left(r_{k_{1}}\ldots r_{k_{j-1}}r_{k_{j+1}}^{\prime}\ldots r_{k_{m}}^{\prime}\right)^{2}

Consider this summand for
j=1
. Note that fixing
s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}
and

k_{1},\ldots,k_{m-1}
, there is at most one choice for the remaining index
k_{m}\in\{1,\ldots,K\}

that satisfies
s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0
. Thus, the summand for
j=1
is at most

\max_{k_{m}=1}^{K}{r_{k_{m}}^{\prime}}^{2}\cdot\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m-1}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\frac{m}{2^{m}}(r_{k_{1}}-r_{k_{1}}^{\prime})^{2}\left(r_{k_{2}}^{\prime}\ldots r_{k_{m-1}}^{\prime}\right)^{2}\leq m\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{\prime})^{2}\bar{r}^{2}R^{2(m-2)}.

The same bound holds for each summand
j=1,\ldots,m
, yielding

\mathrm{I}\leq m^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{\prime})^{2}\bar{r}^{2}R^{2(m-2)}.

To bound
\mathrm{II}
, observe that when
s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0
, we have

\cos\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)=\cos\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}+\alpha\cdot s_{\ell}k_{\ell}\right)

for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
. Then applying
1-\cos(x)\leq x^{2}/2
for any

x\in\mathbb{R}
, we obtain

2\left(1-\cos\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)\right)\leq\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\left(\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}-s_{\ell}\phi_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}+\alpha\cdot s_{\ell}k_{\ell}\right)^{2}=\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}m\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\phi_{k_{j}}-\phi_{k_{j}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{j})^{2}.

So

\mathrm{II}\leq\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\mathbbm{1}\{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0\}\cdot\frac{m}{2^{m}}(\phi_{k_{j}}-\phi_{k_{j}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{j})^{2}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right).

(120)

Applying
\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}^{2}+{r_{k}^{\prime}}^{2})/2\leq R^{2}
and a similar argument as above,
for any fixed
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, this summand for
j=1
is at most

\displaystyle\max_{k_{m}=1}^{K}r_{k_{m}}r_{k_{m}}^{\prime}\cdot\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m-1}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\frac{m}{2^{m}}(\phi_{k_{1}}-\phi_{k_{1}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{1})^{2}\Big(r_{k_{1}}\ldots r_{k_{m-1}}r_{k_{1}}^{\prime}\ldots r_{k_{m-1}}^{\prime}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq m\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}\bar{r}^{2}R^{2(m-2)}.

The same bound holds for each summand
j=1,\ldots,m
, yielding

\mathrm{II}\leq\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}m^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}\cdot\bar{r}^{2}\cdot R^{2(m-2)}.

Combining these bounds for
\mathrm{I}
and
\mathrm{II}
, we arrive at

\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{m}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}\big]\leq m^{2}\bar{r}^{2}\cdot R^{2(m-2)}\cdot\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{\prime})^{2}+r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}.

Let us now apply this to (
117
) and sum over
m\geq 3
:
We have

\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{m^{2}R^{2(m-2)}}{\sigma^{2m}m!}=\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{mR^{2}}{(m-1)(m-2)\sigma^{6}}\cdot\frac{R^{2(m-3)}}{\sigma^{2(m-3)}(m-3)!}\leq\frac{3R^{2}}{2\sigma^{6}}e^{R^{2}/\sigma^{2}}.

Then

\displaystyle\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{e^{\|\theta\|^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}}{\sigma^{2m}m!}\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{m}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\bar{r}^{2}R^{2}e^{3R^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}}{2\sigma^{6}}\cdot\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{\prime})^{2}+r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}.

Applying this and (
118
) to (
117
)
gives (
59
).
∎

For a specific regime of parameters
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2K}
,
we simplify the lower bound for the loss in Proposition

3.1
by expressing the squared distance

|\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha|_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}
on the circle
\mathcal{A}
in terms of the
usual squared distance
(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)^{2}
on
\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Fix any
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2K}
and let

\theta=(r_{k}\cos\phi_{k},r_{k}\sin\phi_{k})_{k=1}^{K}
and

\theta^{\prime}=(r_{k}^{\prime}\cos\phi_{k}^{\prime},r_{k}^{\prime}\sin\phi_{k}^{\prime})_{k=1}^{K}
.
For each
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, let
K_{0}(\alpha)\in[0,K]
be the largest integer for
which
|K_{0}(\alpha)\cdot\alpha|\leq\pi/2
. If
r_{k},r_{k}^{\prime}\geq\underaccent{\bar}{r}

and
|\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}|\leq\pi/3
for each
k=1,\ldots,K
, then
for a universal constant
c>0
,

L(\theta,\theta^{\prime})\geq\sum_{k=1}^{K}(r_{k}-r_{k}^{\prime})^{2}+c\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\left((K-K_{0}(\alpha))\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}(\alpha)}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)^{2}\right)

(121)

where the second summation is understood as 0 if
K_{0}(\alpha)=0
.

Proof.

Recall the form (
10
) of the loss
from Proposition
3.1
,
where the infimum over
\alpha
may be restricted to
[-\pi,\pi]

by periodicity. We provide a lower bound for

\alpha\in[0,\pi]
, and the case
\alpha\in[-\pi,0]
is analogous.

If
\alpha=0
, let
K_{0}=K_{1}=\ldots=K
. Otherwise if
\alpha\in(0,\pi]
,
let
0\leq K_{0}\leq K_{1}\leq K_{2}\leq\ldots
be such that each
K_{m}
is the
largest integer in
[0,K]

for which
K_{m}\cdot\alpha\leq 2\pi m+\frac{\pi}{2}
. Note that if
K_{m}<K

strictly, then we must have also
K_{m}<K_{m+1}
. If
K_{0}\geq 1
, then
for every
k\in[1,K_{0}]
, we have
k\alpha\in[0,\pi/2]
, so

\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha\in[-\pi/3,5\pi/6]
and

1-\cos(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)\geq c(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)^{2}

for a universal constant
c>0
. Thus

\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}\Big[1-\cos(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)\Big]\geq c\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)^{2}.

(122)

This bound is also trivially true if
K_{0}=0
.

Now fix any
m\geq 0
where
K_{m}<K
strictly. Consider the values

k\in\{K_{m}+1,\ldots,K_{m+1}\}.

For each such
k
, we have
k\alpha\in(2\pi m+\frac{\pi}{2},2\pi m+\frac{5\pi}{2}]
.
Let
a
be the number of such values
k
where

k\alpha\in(2\pi m+\frac{\pi}{2},2\pi m+\frac{3\pi}{2}]
, and let

b
be the number of such values
k
where

k\alpha\in(2\pi m+\frac{3\pi}{2},2\pi m+\frac{5\pi}{2}]
.
Then we must have
a\geq 1
because
\alpha\in[0,\pi]
. Also, the number of multiples
of
\alpha
belonging to
(2\pi m+\frac{3\pi}{2},2\pi m+\frac{5\pi}{2}]
is at most 1
more than the number of multiples of
\alpha
belonging to

(2\pi m+\frac{\pi}{2},2\pi m+\frac{3\pi}{2}]
, so
b\leq a+1
. Thus

\frac{a}{K_{m+1}-K_{m}}=\frac{a}{a+b}\geq\frac{a}{2a+1}\geq\frac{1}{3}.

For
k=K_{m}+1,\ldots,K_{m}+a
, we must have
\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha\in(2\pi m+\frac{\pi}{6},2\pi m+\frac{11\pi}{6}]
, so

1-\cos(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)\geq c

for a universal constant
c>0
. Then

\sum_{k=K_{m}+1}^{K_{m+1}}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}\Big[1-\cos(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)\Big]\geq\sum_{k=K_{m}+1}^{K_{m}+a}c\cdot r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}\geq c\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}\cdot\frac{K_{m+1}-K_{m}}{3}.

Now summing over all
m\geq 0
where
K_{m}<K
,

\sum_{k=K_{0}+1}^{K}r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}\Big[1-\cos(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+k\alpha)\Big]\geq\frac{c\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}}{3}\cdot(K-K_{0}).

(123)

Applying (
122
) and (
123
) and the analogous
bounds for
\alpha\in[-\pi,0]
to (
10
),
and taking the infimum over
\alpha\in[-\pi,\pi]
, we obtain
(
121
).
∎

We conclude the proof of Lemma
6.1
for
\beta=0

using the following version of Assouad’s hypercube lower bound from

(
Cai and Zhou 2012
, Lemma 2)
.

Lemma C.2
.

Fix
m\geq 1
, let
\{P_{\tau}:\tau\in\{0,1\}^{m}\}

be any
2^{m}
probability distributions, and let
\psi(P_{\tau})

take values in a metric space with metric
d
. Then for any
s>0

and any estimator
\hat{\psi}(X)
based on
X\sim P_{\tau}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in\{0,1\}^{m}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim P_{\tau}}\Big[d(\hat{\psi}(X),\psi(P_{\tau}))^{s}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{m}{2^{s+1}}\cdot\min_{H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\geq 1}\frac{d(\psi(P_{\tau}),\psi(P_{\tau^{\prime}}))^{s}}{H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})}\cdot\min_{H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=1}\Big(1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_{\tau},P_{\tau^{\prime}})\Big).

Here,
H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbbm{1}\{\tau_{i}\neq\tau_{i}^{\prime}\}
is the Hamming
distance between
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
, and
D_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_{\tau},P_{\tau^{\prime}})
is the
total-variation distance between
P_{\tau}
and
P_{\tau^{\prime}}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
,
\beta=0
.

We define
2^{K}
parameters
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{P}_{0}

indexed by
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{K}
: Fix a value
\phi\in[0,\pi/3]
to be
determined. For each
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{K}
, set

\phi_{k}^{\tau}=\tau_{k}\phi=\begin{cases}\phi&\text{ if }\tau_{k}=1\\
0&\text{ if }\tau_{k}=0.\end{cases}

(124)

Then let
\theta^{\tau}
be the vector where

r_{k}(\theta)=1
and
\phi_{k}(\theta)=\phi_{k}^{\tau}
for each
k=1,\ldots,K
.

Let
P_{\tau}=p_{\theta^{\tau}}^{N}
denote the law of
N

samples
y^{(1)},\ldots,y^{(N)}\overset{iid}{\sim}p_{\theta^{\tau}}
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{\theta}=\{g(\alpha)\cdot\theta:\alpha\in\mathcal{A}\}
be the rotational
orbit of
\theta
. Then

d(\mathcal{O}_{\theta},\mathcal{O}_{\theta^{\prime}}):=L(\theta,\theta^{\prime})^{1/2}=\min_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}}\|\theta^{\prime}-g(\alpha)\cdot\theta\|

defines a metric over the space of all such orbits. We apply
Lemma
C.2
with
m=K
,
\psi(P_{\tau})=\mathcal{O}_{\theta^{\tau}}
,
this metric
d(\mathcal{O}_{\theta},\mathcal{O}_{\theta^{\prime}})
, and
s=2
. Applying
(
58
) and
|e^{is}-e^{it}|\leq|s-t|
for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
,

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})\leq\frac{\|\theta^{\tau}-\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\big|e^{i\phi_{k}^{\tau}}-e^{i\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}}}\big|^{2}\leq\frac{\phi^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\cdot H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(125)

where
H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})
is the Hamming distance.
Applying (
59
) with the right side evaluated at

\alpha=0
, where
\bar{r}=1
and
R^{2}=K
, also

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})\leq\frac{\phi^{2}}{A}\cdot H(\tau,\tau^{\prime}),\qquad A:=\frac{2\sigma^{6}}{3Ke^{3K/2\sigma^{2}}}

(126)

Then, setting

\phi=\min\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\max(\sqrt{2\sigma^{2}},\sqrt{A}),\frac{\pi}{3}\right),

(127)

these bounds imply for both cases of the max that

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})\leq H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})/N
.
Then by Pinsker’s
inequality (see e.g.
(
Tsybakov 2008
, Lemma 2.5)
),

D_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_{\tau},P_{\tau^{\prime}})\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(P_{\tau}\|P_{\tau^{\prime}})}=\sqrt{\frac{N}{2}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})}\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})},

so

\min_{H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=1}\Big(1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_{\tau},P_{\tau^{\prime}})\Big)\geq 1-\sqrt{1/2}>0.

Since
\phi_{k}\in[0,\pi/3]
for every
k
, we may apply
Lemma
C.1
to lower-bound the loss: For a universal
constant
c>0
, we have

L(\theta^{\tau},\theta^{\tau^{\prime}})\geq c\inf_{K_{0}\in[0,K]}\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\left(K-K_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}}+k\alpha)^{2}\right).

(128)

For any fixed
K_{0}\in[2,K]
, the inner infimum over
\alpha
is
attained at
\alpha=-\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}})/\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k^{2}
, and we have

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}}+k\alpha)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}})^{2}-\frac{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}})\right)^{2}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k^{2}}

\displaystyle=\phi^{2}\cdot\left(H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})-\frac{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k}^{\prime})\right)^{2}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k^{2}}\right)

where
\tau^{K_{0}}=(\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{K_{0}})
,

{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}}=(\tau_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\tau_{K_{0}}^{\prime})
, and
H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})

is the Hamming distance of these subvectors in
\{0,1\}^{K_{0}}
.
Subject to a constraint that
H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})=h
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k}^{\prime})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(K_{0}+(K_{0}-1)+\ldots+(K_{0}-h+1)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{h(2K_{0}-h+1)}{2}\right)^{2}=h\cdot\frac{h(2K_{0}-h+1)^{2}}{4}\leq h\cdot\frac{(2K_{0}+1)^{3}}{27},

where the last inequality is tight at the maximizer
h=(2K_{0}+1)/3
. Then
for any
K_{0}\in[2,K]
,

\displaystyle H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})-\frac{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k}^{\prime})\right)^{2}}{\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}k^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})\cdot\left(1-\frac{(2K_{0}+1)^{3}/27}{K_{0}(K_{0}+1)(2K_{0}+1)/6}\right)\geq\frac{2H(\tau^{K_{0}},{\tau^{\prime}}^{K_{0}})}{27}.

Applying also
K-K_{0}\geq H((\tau_{K_{0}+1},\ldots,\tau_{K}),(\tau_{K_{0}+1}^{\prime},\ldots,\tau_{K}^{\prime}))

and
\phi\leq\pi/3
, we get

\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\left(K-K_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{K_{0}}(\phi_{k}^{\tau}-\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}}+k\alpha)^{2}\right)\geq c\phi^{2}H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(129)

for a universal constant
c>0
. For
K_{0}=0
or
K_{0}=1
(and any
K\geq 2
), we
may instead lower bound the left side by
K-K_{0}\geq K/2\geq H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})/2
,
so that this bound (
129
) holds also. Thus, taking the
infimum in (
128
) over
K_{0}\in[0,K]
,

d(\psi(P_{\tau}),\psi(P_{\tau^{\prime}}))^{2}=L(\theta^{\tau},\theta^{\tau^{\prime}})\geq c^{\prime}\phi^{2}\cdot H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

(130)

for a universal constant
c^{\prime}>0
. Applying Lemma
C.2

with
m=K
and
s=2
, we obtain

\inf_{\hat{\theta}}\sup_{\theta^{*}\in\mathcal{P}(r)}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}[L(\theta^{*},\hat{\theta})]\geq\inf_{\hat{\theta}}\sup_{\theta^{\tau}:\tau\in\{0,1\}^{K}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{\tau}}[L(\theta^{\tau},\hat{\theta})]\geq c^{\prime\prime}K\phi^{2}.

(131)

Applying the form of
\phi
from (
127
) concludes the proof.
∎

We now extend this argument to the more general case of
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
. Consider the subset of
\mathcal{P}_{\beta}
defined by

\mathcal{P}_{\beta}^{*}=\Big\{\theta^{*}\in\mathbb{R}^{2K}:r_{k}(\theta^{*})=k^{-\beta}\text{ for all }k\in\{1,\ldots,K\}~~\text{and}~~\phi_{k}(\theta^{*})=0\text{ for
all }k\leq K/2\Big\}

where the phases of the first half of the Fourier frequencies are fixed to 0.
For
\theta,\theta^{\prime}
belonging to
\mathcal{P}_{\beta}^{*}
, the following lemma
modifies the KL upper bound (
59
) from
Lemma
6.2
, replacing the factor

\bar{r}=\max_{k}r_{k}=1
by a multiple of
\underaccent{\bar}{r}=K^{-2\beta}
.

Lemma C.3
.

Fix any
\beta\in[0,\frac{1}{2})
, and denote

R^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{-2\beta}
and
\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}=K^{-2\beta}
.
Then for all
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta}^{*}
,

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})\leq\frac{27\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}R^{2}e^{5R^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}}{\sigma^{6}}\cdot\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{-2\beta}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}

(132)

Proof.

Following the proof of Lemma
6.2
, we provide a new bound for
the quantity
\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}
in (
119
).

Since
r_{k}(\theta)=r_{k}(\theta^{\prime})
for all
k
,
we have
\mathrm{I}=0
. For
\mathrm{II}
, notice that we may restrict the
summation over
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}
in its definition to tuples where

\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})>K/2
, since otherwise the summand is 0 upon setting

\alpha=0
, by the condition
\phi_{k}(\theta)=\phi_{k}(\theta^{\prime})
for all

k\leq K/2
. Therefore, we have similarly to (
120
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathop{\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}}_{\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})>K/2}^{K}\;\;\mathop{\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}}_{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0}\frac{m}{2^{m}}(\phi_{k_{j}}-\phi_{k_{j}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{j})^{2}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right).

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, consider the summand for
j=1
, and notice
that if
s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0
and
\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})>K/2
, then
the second largest index amongst
k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}
is at least
K/(2m)
.
Then, the summand for
j=1
is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{i=2}^{m}\mathop{\mathop{\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}}_{\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})>K/2}}_{k_{i}\geq\max(k_{2},\ldots,k_{m})}\;\;\mathop{\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}}_{s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0}\frac{m}{2^{m}}(\phi_{k_{1}}-\phi_{k_{1}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{1})^{2}\left(\prod_{\ell=1}^{m}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=2}^{m}\;\max_{k_{i}>\frac{K}{2m}}r_{k_{i}}r_{k_{i}}^{\prime}\;\sum_{k_{1},\ldots,k_{i-1},k_{i+1},\ldots,k_{m}=1}^{K}\sum_{s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}\in\{+1,-1\}}\frac{m}{2^{m}}(\phi_{k_{1}}-\phi_{k_{1}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{1})^{2}\left(\prod_{\ell\neq i}r_{k_{\ell}}r_{k_{\ell}}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\leq m^{2}r^{2}\left(\frac{K}{2m}\right)^{-2\beta}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{K}r_{k_{1}}r_{k_{1}}^{\prime}(\phi_{k_{1}}-\phi_{k_{1}}^{\prime}+\alpha k_{1})^{2}R^{2(m-2)}.

In the second line, for each fixed
i\in\{2,\ldots,m\}
,
we have used that for every choice of

s_{1},\ldots,s_{m}
and
k_{1},\ldots,k_{i-1},k_{i+1},\ldots,k_{m}
, there is at most
one choice of
k_{i}
for which conditions
s_{1}k_{1}+\ldots+s_{m}k_{m}=0
,

\max(k_{1},\ldots,k_{m})>K/2
, and
k_{i}\geq\max(k_{2},\ldots,k_{m})
all hold, and
such a value
k_{i}
must be at least
K/(2m)
. The same bound holds for each
summand
j=1,\ldots,m
. Then, applying
(K/2m)^{-2\beta}=(2m)^{2\beta}\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}<2m\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}
for
\beta<1/2
,
and taking the infimum over
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
, this gives

\mathrm{II}\leq\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}2m^{4}R^{2(m-2)}\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{-2\beta}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}.

Thus for all
m\geq 3
,

\mathbb{E}[\langle\theta,g\theta\rangle^{m}-2\langle\theta,g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}+\langle\theta^{\prime},g\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{m}]\leq 2m^{4}R^{2(m-2)}\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}\cdot\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{-2\beta}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2},

and the left side is 0 for
m=0,1,2
because

r_{k}(\theta)=r_{k}(\theta^{\prime})
for all
k
.

Now we apply this to (
117
) and sum over
m\geq 3
, using

\displaystyle\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{2m^{4}R^{2(m-2)}}{\sigma^{2m}m!}\leq\max_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{2m^{2}R^{2}}{(m-1)(m-2)\sigma^{6}}\cdot\sum_{m=3}^{\infty}\frac{m\cdot R^{2(m-3)}}{\sigma^{2(m-3)}(m-3)!}=\frac{9R^{2}}{\sigma^{6}}\cdot\left(\frac{R^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+3\right)e^{R^{2}/\sigma^{2}}\leq\frac{27R^{2}}{\sigma^{6}}e^{2R^{2}/\sigma^{2}}

This yields as desired

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})\leq\chi^{2}(p_{\theta}\|p_{\theta^{\prime}})\leq\frac{27R^{2}}{\sigma^{6}}e^{5R^{2}/2\sigma^{2}}\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}\cdot\inf_{\alpha\in\mathbb{R}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}k^{-2\beta}(\phi_{k}-\phi_{k}^{\prime}+\alpha k)^{2}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Consider the subset of points
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{K}
such that
\tau_{k}=0
for all

k\leq K/2
. Fixing a value
\phi\in[0,\pi/3]
, we define corresponding to
each such
\tau\in\{0,1\}^{K}
the parameter
\theta^{\tau}\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta}^{*}
where

r_{k}(\theta)=k^{-\beta}
and
\phi_{k}(\theta)=\tau_{k}\phi
for each

k=1,\ldots,K
. These parameter vectors
\theta^{\tau}
are thus identified with
the vertices of a hypercube of dimension
m=\lfloor K/2\rfloor
.

For any two such vectors
\theta^{\tau}
and
\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}
,
applying (
58
), we have
analogously to (
125
) that

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})\leq\frac{\|\theta^{\tau}-\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}\|^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}=\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\sum_{k>K/2}k^{-2\beta}\big|e^{i\phi_{k}^{\tau}}-e^{i\phi_{k}^{\tau^{\prime}}}\big|^{2}\leq\frac{\phi^{2}}{K^{2\beta}\sigma^{2}}\cdot H(\tau,\tau^{\prime}),

the last inequality using
(K/2)^{-2\beta}<2K^{-2\beta}
when
\beta<1/2
.
Applying (
132
) with
\alpha=0
,
where
\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}=K^{-2\beta}
and
R^{2}=CK^{1-2\beta}
,
we have analogously to (
126
) that for constants
C,c>0

depending only on
\beta
,

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\theta^{\tau}}\|p_{\theta^{\tau^{\prime}}})\leq\frac{\phi^{2}}{A}\cdot H(\tau,\tau^{\prime}),\qquad A:=\frac{c\sigma^{6}K^{4\beta}}{K^{1-2\beta}e^{CK^{1-2\beta}/2\sigma^{2}}}

Lower bounding both
\underaccent{\bar}{r}^{2}
and
r_{k}r_{k}^{\prime}
by
K^{-2\beta}
in
Lemma
C.1
, and applying (
129
), we have

L(\theta^{\tau},\theta^{\tau^{\prime}})\geq c^{\prime}K^{-2\beta}\phi^{2}H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})

for a universal constant
c^{\prime}>0
. We can choose

\phi=\min\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\cdot\max(\sqrt{K^{2\beta}\sigma^{2}},\sqrt{A}),\frac{\pi}{3}\right)

to ensure
1-D_{\mathrm{TV}}(P_{\tau},P_{\tau^{\prime}})\geq 1-\sqrt{1/2}>0
whenever

H(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=1
, as before. Finally, by using Lemma
C.2

with
m=\lfloor K/2\rfloor
, we can conclude analogously to
(
131
) that

\inf_{\hat{\theta}}\sup_{\theta^{*}\in\mathcal{P}_{\beta}^{*}}\mathbb{E}_{\theta^{*}}[L(\theta^{*},\hat{\theta})]\geq c^{\prime\prime}K^{1-2\beta}\phi^{2},

and applying the above choice of
\phi
concludes the proof.
∎
